               Hewing table highlSpaceLogic C-Bus Classroom Training

                                     v1.2 April 2026

The intention of this workbook is to complement the Introduction to C-Bus Online Module,
helping to reinforce the learning objectives and maximizing your learning experience.

This workbook contains exercises designed to help you become familiar with the SpaceLogic
C-Bus Commission software environment and how it can be used to commission C-Bus
Dimmers, Relays, Saturn & Saturn Zen Wall Plates, Controllers and DALI.
Revision History
The following table highlights key changes to this workbook based on feedback and recommended
changes from key stakeholders:

 Version        Date                Summary of change/s
 1.2            April 2026          Added additional information to Firmware Upgrade chapter
 1.1            January 2026        Added chapter of FAQ's
 1.0            October 2025        N/A

SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission

Creating Project
There are several methods to create a project in SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission; fundamentally by
creating a completely new project in the software or by importing a project:




                               Figure 1: SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission Create Project

Projects created in C-Bus Toolkit will also appear in SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission, this is because
both software applications share the same driver (and hence project database) in C-Gate 3:




                                                  Figure 2: C-Gate 3

When SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission is opened you will be navigated to the home page, displaying all
projects:




                                   Figure 3: SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission Home




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property        Page | 8
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Software Environment
When a SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission project is opened, the Project Space is presented:




                               Figure 4: SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission Project Space

The layout of the Project can be reset through the Settings Menu:




                                            Figure 5: Reset Window Layout




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property     Page | 9
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
The Project Space for a project can be customised using up to 5 windows:

     Windows                                                        Description
                       This window allows you to view and manage all the networks created in the current project. Clicking
      Explorer
                       on a project's node in the Explorer window will display additional sub-nodes

      Library          This window allows you to view the software's library of devices

                       This window is the main area and displays the contents of the selected node from the Explorer
                       window. When the C-Bus Devices node is selected, the workspace consists of two sections:

                       Devices in projects
                       This section displays all the devices in the project's database
    Workspace
                       Network Devices
                       This section displays the devices physically connected to the C-Bus Network (provided a C-Bus
                       Interface is connected to the C-Bus Network and the software can communicate with it)
                       *For the SpaceLogic C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway, this section becomes Line Devices, and displays the
                       connected DALI devices, provided the gateway is part of the C-Bus Network

     Properties        This window allows you to view the applicable properties of a selected device

    Deployment         This window lists all live network related activities, including displaying the process of devices
      Queue            being extracted to the Devices in Project section or deployed to the Network devices section.


                                            Figure 6: Project Space Windows

Windows can be Closed or Opened by clicking the drop-down arrow next to Windows in the top menu
bar:




                                            Figure 7: Project Space Windows

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

however, the window cannot be viewed unless its tab is clicked:




                                             Figure 8: Deployment Queue

The Workspace window will only Open/Appear in the middle section of the Project Space:




                                                 Figure 9: Workspace

The appearance of your Project Space can be customised by modifying the size of height or width of
your windows. This can be done by hovering your mouse over borders of a window.

When hovering over the left or right border of a window, your mouse will change to the following
cursor:


You can then increase or reduce the height or width of the window by clicking, holding and dragging
the cursor:




                                         Figure 10: Increase or Reduce height

The appearance of your Project Space can be customised by modifying the layout of your windows,
where the position of a window can be changed using a snap-to-grid like feature across different
sections:




                                          Figure 11: Guides to Dock Window




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property          Page | 12
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
You can modify a Window by hovering over its title bar and right clicking or clicking the drop-down
arrow to display a menu of control options:
    • Float: this control option allows you to float a window, undocking it from its
    • Dock: this control option allows you to dock a floating window into its previous position in the
       project space, excluding the middle section*
    • Dock as Document: this control option allows you to dock a window into the middle section of
       the project space only, which is designated specifically for the Workspace window
    • Auto hide: this control option will minimise the window/s into the left/right or top/bottom of
       the project space, depending on their location. Windows are then accessed via tabs that are
       displayed in the project space
    • Close: this control option will close the window, which can then be re-enabled by the Windows
       drop-down menu from the top menu bar

*The Dock option will not dock a window into the section designated specifically for the Workspace
window




                             Figure 12: Right Click on a window to show control options




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property          Page | 13
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
The Deployment Queue window is docked in the same window as the Properties window, allowing
you to swap between the two using tabs.

For this workbook, the term Multi-window will be used to define the situation where:

One or more windows are docked together in the same window and only one window is displayed at a
time, and can be switched using their respective tabs*




                                             Figure 13: Multiple windows

*For a Multi-window, the tabs to access each window are found at the bottom; however, the tabs are
found at the top of the window when docked in the middle section of the project space, and is known
by another term

Firmware Upgrade
In SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission, the firmware of a unit can be upgraded by clicking Firmware in the
Menu bar:




                                             Figure 19: Firmware Upgrade


The Firmware Upgrade dialog box displays devices that are currently connected to the programming
laptop/computer via its USB-A port connections:




                                             Figure 20: Firmware Upgrade

next to a unit, highlighting that a firmware upgrade is available:

General Rules and Recommendations

Simultaneous use with C-Bus Toolkit
The following are general rules and recommendations to help guide you on using both SpaceLogic
C-Bus Commission and C-Bus Toolkit at the same time:

1.

Use one application at a time if possible

[
i

NRIR

Figure 24: Use one application at a time if possible

2. When using both applications, perform activities one at a time

|
i

il

Figure 25: When using both applications, perform activities one at a time

Working parallel on the project is more forgiving; however:

* It may be necessary to refresh your project

* You can only edit one unit/device at a time (close the Unit dialog box ,clear/unload the
Properties window)
Working concurrently with both software applications on the same live network is more
problematic and must be avoided:

» Perform one live network activity at a time; for example, deployment/transfer, readdress,
loading/editing devices on the live network from one software application (C-Bus Toolkit or
SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission) at one time

* You may experience a device lock, as C-Gate will not allow things to happen in parallel

Error x

e Device Not Ready (Locked)

The device is not ready for this operation. Please try again. Also ensure there is no other instance of C-Bus software accessing the
same project, network or device.

OK

Figure 26: Device Not Ready

SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission does not support a native reset unit/feature option; however, there is a
way to set a unit/device back to a default state using devices added from the Library. This will be
demonstrated by setting a previously commissioned Digital Dimmer back to a default state:

   1. In the example below, the C-Bus network has been opened & scanned, with a Digital Dimmer
      that has already been commissioned:




                        Figure 28: SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission - C-Bus Network scanned

   2. The same digital dimmer will be searched & added from the Library to Devices in Project:




                                    Figure 29: Digital Dimmer added from Library

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
  3. The project device needs to be readdressed to match the network device:




                                      Figure 30: Readdress to Match Network

  4. Once addresses match, select both devices:




                                          Figure 31: Both devices selected




© 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property   Page | 24
of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

     network device:




                                         Figure 32: Deploy selected devices

Initial State: Setup
Before turning the demo case on, the following needs to be setup:

Initial State: C-Bus Power Supply
When the demo case is initially powered on, the C-Bus units may not respond or behave as expected,
this is because the C-Bus power for the C-Bus network in the case will be provided by the SpaceLogic
C-Bus Digital Dimmer & Voltage Free Relay; however, these in-built C-Bus power supply are disabled
by default*.

*This is highlighted by a Solid Yellow Unit LED Indicator

To help ensure the C-Bus units function correctly, the in-built C-Bus power supplies on both units need
to be enabled. For the 4-Channel Digital Dimmer and Voltage Free Relay, Press & Hold the
Channel 1 LED Indicator button and then Short Press the Channel 4 LED Indicator button*:




                                    Figure 38: Enable in-built C-Bus power supply

*The Unit LED Indicator will display Solid Green to highlight that the in-built C-Bus power supply has
been enabled

Initial State: C-Bus Clock
It is a recommended practice to have 3 enabled C-Bus Clocks on a network, where an internal
algorithm will decide which clock will be active, remaining C-Bus clocks will remain idle. The following
units in the case support a C-Bus Clock:
     • 4-Channel Digital Dimmer
     • 4-Channel Voltage Free Relay
     • SpaceLogic C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway
     • SpaceLogic C-Bus Network Automation Controller

The C-Bus Clock on both the 4-Channel Digital Dimmer and Voltage Free Relay can be enabled by
pressing & holding the Channel 1 LED Indicator button and then short pressing the Channel 3 LED
Indicator button*:




                                            Figure 39: Enable C-Bus Clock

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Initial State: C-Bus Hardware Network Burden
A C-Bus Network Burden is a resistor and capacitor circuit that is placed across the positive and
negative C-Bus pairs. Its purpose is to provide the C-Bus network with a standard impedance, helping
to ensure communications are stable and reliable.

The C-Bus network in the demo needs a network burden to help ensure communications are stable
and reliable, this is achieved through the provided Hardware Network Burden (5500BUR).




                                     Figure 50: C-Bus Network Burden connected


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property      Page | 35
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Bus Interface device that is not defined in the project, this allows:
     • Temporary access to a local C-Bus Network or bridged C-Bus Network without the need to
         change the originally set interface
     • Access to a C-Bus bridge network, helping for faster commissioning without compromising the
         network topology

Dimmers & Relays

Dimmers: Channel Confirmation
By default, all channels of digital dimmers are set to Trailing Edge dimming mode and can detect the
connected load. The correct mode of each channel needs to be tested and confirmed based on the
connected load type before the unit is used.

On the digital dimmers, you can check the operation of the connected load by short pressing the
channel button to toggle the state of the channel. The load should turn on, and the channel button will
illuminate in the colour corresponding to its currently selected dimming mode:
    • Trailing Edge = Yellow
    • Leading Edge = Green

By default, all channels support a Load Detection Mode, helping to detect the connected load type;
the dimming mode then needs to be confirmed for each channel based on this load type. The process
to confirm the mode is:

    1. Turn ON the channel by short pressing the channel button:




                                Figure 73: Short press Channel 1 LED Indicator button

If the channel indicator is:

    •   Solid Yellow: the load is compatible with Trailing Edge mode, confirm that the mode selection
        is correct by double-pressing the channel button. The indicator will flash yellow rapidly to
        confirm that Trailing Edge mode is selected, completing the process for the channel

    •   Flashing Slow Red: an iron-core load is detected. Change the channel to Leading Edge
        mode by pressing and holding the channel button for longer than 10s. The channel indicator
        will flash Green rapidly to confirm that the Leading Edge mode is selected, completing the
        process for the channel.

        If after changing to Leading Edge mode, the indicator continues to flash Red, the default
        dimmer load profile is not suitable with the connected load and needs to be customised using
        SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission

    The Dimmer Channels need to be powered for the channel/s to be tested & confirmed via the front
    panel or SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 46
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
There are two main situations when it is recommended to confirming the dimming mode of each
channel in the digital dimmer:

¢ Fresh out of the Box: It is recommended to confirm the dimming mode of each channel when
you use a digital dimmer that is straight out of the box. Recall, that by default, all channels are
set to Load Detection Mode, the correct mode needs to be confirmed for each channel based
on the connected load type.

+ After changing Dimming Mode back to Trailing Edge: The dimming mode of each digital
dimmer channel can be changed using either the dimmer’s front panel or SpaceLogic C-Bus
Commission. If a channel is ever changed back to Trailing Edge dimming, it is recommended
to re-confirm the dimming mode.

You can confirm the dimming mode of Channel 1 of the Digital Dimmer in the demo case:

Dimmers: Warn Before Off
Each channel of the digital dimmer supports a Warn Before Off feature that functions as follows:
   • An off command is sent to the channel's programmed group address (such as using a wall
      switch)
   • The group address is set to off (0 - 0%) but the load wired to the channel remains on at a
      defined level for a defined time
   • Once this defined time expires the load will turn off

The Warn Before Off feature for Channel 1 of the Digital Dimmer will be setup so that when the group
address, LED Strip, is set to off (0 - 0%), the load will:
   • Go to 20%
   • Remain on for an additional minute before turning off

   1. Click the project digital dimmer to load it into the Properties window.
   2. For the Channel 1 parameter expand the Advanced parameter:




                                           Figure 108: Advanced parameter




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property       Page | 66
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
  3. Click the Warn Before Off Time drop-down arrow and select 1 minute
  4. Click the Warn Before Off Level drop-down arrow and select 20%




                                     Figure 109: Warn Before Off Time & Level

  5. At the bottom of the Properties window, ensure that the Deploy to Network checkbox is
     enabled and click Save to deploy the changes to the project and network devices.
  6. The Warn Before Off functionality is now implemented for the physical LED strip in the demo
     case and can be experienced by toggling Key 1 of the Saturn Zen white wall plate.



              It is recommended to disable the Warn Before Off after you have verified its
                                       functionality & behaviour




© 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property          Page | 67
of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
The Warn Before Off feature for the digital dimmer can be enabled or disabled using an Enable
Control group address, programmable in the Warn Before Off category:




                                      Figure 110: Warn Before Off Enable Group

using SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission.

Custom Load Profile And Dimming Curve x

Configuration  Testing ‘

A Load Profile adjusts certain settings within each dimmer channel which tailors the dimming behavior to improve the end to end dimming experience.

Inbuilt Load Profiles and the Inbuilt Dimming Curve contain preconfigured settings which cannot be modified. If acceptable results cannot be realized with the load being used, the settings can
be customized and then applied to a dimmer channel. A Custom File can also be saved and used within other channels of the dimmer and also shared across other compatible dimmer devices.

Note: The Load Brand, Load Model, Load Quantity, Custom Notes, Load Type and Mains Frequency fields are saved as project settings only. Furthermore, the Load Type and Mains Frequency
selections determine the values presented in the Custom Load Profile section.

Adjusting the dimming range helps to optimise the dimming operation of the load/s wired to the
channel, helping to reduce erratic behaviour at low brightness levels, such as flickering. At max
brightness levels, it can also help to mask errors of low response




                       Figure 118: Minimum & Maximum Brightness and Dimming Behaviour

The Custom Load Profile and Dimming Curve window also supports a Testing tab, helping to reduce
time spent on achieving the desired dimming behaviour.

Dimming range testing is done over a live C-Bus Network in a real time session between the software
and the dimmer; therefore, the Testing tab is disabled if the following conditions are not met:
   • The C-Bus Network is opened & scanned
   • The channel has a group address assigned
   • If you are editing a dimmer from your project there must be a corresponding live unit, with
       the same group address in an opened & scanned C-Bus Network

trying different minimum brightness values, it can sometimes be necessary to leave the load off for a

Voltage Free Relays: Interlock Channels
When relay channels are interlocked they are unable to be switched on at the same time, the channels
of a voltage free relay can be programmed for interlocking through SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission.

The 2 Channels of the voltage free relay will now be interlocked based on the following priority:
   • Highest Priority: Channel1
   • Lowest Priority: Channel2

   1. In C-Bus Devices, click the project voltage free relay to load it into the Properties window.
   2. Navigate to and expand the Interlock Channels category:




                                            Figure 146: Interlock Channels

   3. Click the Add Interlock Set Add Icon:




                                        Figure 147: Add Interlock Set Add Icon

   4. An Interlock Set will be added, leave the Number of Channels to Interlock field set to 2.



 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 86
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
   5. Click the Highest Priority drop-down arrow and select Channel1.




                                       Figure 148: Interlock Set Highest Priority

   6. Click the Lowest Priority drop-down arrow and select Channel2.




                                       Figure 149: Interlock Set Lowest Priority

   7. At the bottom of the Properties window, ensure that the Deploy to Network checkbox is
      enabled and click Save to deploy the changes to the project and network devices.

Channel 1 and Channel 2 of the voltage free relay are now interlocked, as Channel 1 has the
highest priority, Channel 2 will not turn on if Channel 1 is on.

This completes an example of programming interlocking channels for a voltage free relay in
SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission.


               It is recommended to remove this Interlocking Set after you have verified its
                                       functionality & behaviour

A distinct programming feature of the Saturn & Saturn Zen Wall Plates is the concept and use of
Widgets. This training and workbook will define the term Widget as:

A widget is a way of presenting the programming of a function by placing all the
relevant application parameters into one location in the wall plate, where you can
add/remove widgets from each key.

As of the creation of this training module, the available Widgets are:
* Lighting

* Timer

* Shutter Relay

* Enable

* Fan Cycle

* Scene

» Sequence

* Linked

* Multi-Toggle

As future versions of SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission are released, more Widgets
may become available

Each Wall Plate supports a total of 16 Widgets, which can be programmed into each of its Keys. For
each Key, a single, or multiple Widgets can be added (up to all 16), or removed.

Each of the 16 Widgets consists of two categories:
   • Type/function: where you can select each Widget's type such as Lighting, Linked Widget or
       Sequence
   • Indicator Colour: where you can assign an on/off indicator colour to each Widget

For each Key in a wall plate you can:
   • Add / Remove a single or multiple Widget Type/function
   • Assign an Indicator to a Widget without any bearing on the Widget Type/function

        The behaviour of the Indicator is dependent on any group address that has been assigned to
        the Widget as part of the Type/function.

Wall Plates: Scenes
The Saturn & Saturn Zen Wall Plates support up to 8 Scenes, with storage for a total of up to 64
Scene Items, which can be shared freely amongst the desired number of Scenes.

For larger scenes, or where it may be desirable for the end user to edit the scene contents, it is
recommended defining the scenes in an Automation Controller, and using the Key Unit to send the
Scene Trigger.

The Wall Plates can have:
   • Multiple Lighting Applications per Scene
   • Ramp rates specified per Scene Item
   • Multiple Trigger Groups per key unit
   • Scene Toggle and Scene Dim Key Functions (these Key Functions only work on Scenes which
      are contained in the unit in which the scene is defined)

Scene is available as a Widget, offering the scene Key Function of Scene Set, complemented with
new scene related functions such as:

   Key Function        Description

                       If scene is not already active, a short press triggers the scene
   Scene Toggle
                       If scene is already active, a short press turns off all groups in the scene that are on

     Scene Set         Short press triggers scene

                       If scene is active, a short press turns off all groups in the scene that are on
     Scene Off
                       No effect if scene is inactive

                       If scene is active, a short press turns off all groups in the scene that are on
  Scene Off/Down       If scene is active, a long press ramps all groups in the scene down at the specified ramp rate
                       No effect if scene is inactive

                       A Short Press triggers the scene
   Scene On/Up
                       If scene is active, a long press ramps all groups in the scene up at the specified ramp rate


Scenes in the Wall Plate are programmed using the Scene Manager that is accessible under the
Global category:




                                       Figure 173: Wall Plate - Devices Scenes

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Wall Plates: Profiles

Profiles allow the function of each key to be changed at run-time, so that the action of any or all of the
keys can be different at different times of the day. This can also be used to replicate the Join Mode
functionality of older key units.

An example of this could be having the following Profiles for a building:
o Day Mode
e After Hours
¢ Function Room
¢ Cleaning

Each of the wall plates can support up to 4 Profiles, which can be selected/changed using the Levels
of an assigned Enable Control Group Address:

¢ Level (0-0%) — Profile 1
e Level (1) — Profile 2
e Level (2) — Profile 3
¢ Level (3) — Profile 4

Once loaded, the programming of each Profile will be loaded into the Wall Plate, including Indicator
Colours. Profiles will now be created for the Saturn Zen White Wall Plate, each with its own purpose
for this training:

¢ Level (0 - 0%) Profile 1 — Dimmer & Scenes

o Level (1) Profile 2 — Sequences

e Level (2) Profile 3 — Timer

¢ Level (3) Profile 4 — Linked

Wall Plates: Sequences
Sequences are similar to Scenes; however, rather than sending all the changes at once, they
deliberately send them out as a sequence of events. This allows inclusion of commands from C-Bus
Applications other than Lighting (such as Audio), as well as the introduction of delays between
commands.

This can be used to:
   • Create lighting effects such as turning a sequence of lights on in a specific order, with a delay
       in between, and then turning them off in the reverse order
   • To allow time for other systems to react before performing the next action; for example,
       allowing time for a projector screen to completely lower before dimming the lights in a home
       theatre

In a similar way to Scenes, Sequences can be triggered by a key event, or by a Trigger message on
the Trigger application.


                    Like Scenes, Sequences that share a Trigger Group are considered
                                         Mutually Exclusive


Scenes in the Wall Plate are programmed using the Sequence Manager that is accessible under the
Global category:




                                            Figure 218: Sequence Manager

A sequence will now be created as part of the Sequences profile that was highlighted earlier.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 126
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Wall Plates: Timer
The Saturn & Saturn Zen Wall Plates support the capability to program Timer functionality as part of
a Widget.

The Timer Widget provides timer functions for Lighting Groups; for example, turning a group on for 10
minutes and then turning it off.

It implements the traditional Toggle Timer and Retrigger Timer behaviours, which work the same
way as previous key units, but offer more flexibility with the ability to specify both the initial and expiry
levels as well as the initial and final ramp rates.

There are also several new types of Timer functions:
o The Delay Timer starts a timer but does not emit an initial event, allowing a delayed event
without affecting the current state of the Group.

* The Pulse Timer is similar to a normal “Retrigger Timer”, but the initial event is always emitted,
regardless of the Group state.

 The Toggle Timer with On works the same as the Toggle Timer, but also allows the Group to
be turned on without a timer by using a long press. This is useful for example a light in a room
such as a pantry or a laundry where you normally want a timer to automatically switch the light
off after a few minutes, but occasionally you need the light to stay on.

The specific behaviour of the Timer Widget is selected by choosing the desired Key Function.

If the unit is not currently the Active unit for the group, of if the widget level and the current
target level of the group are different, the initial event is generated

At the expiry of the timer, the expiry event is generated

If the group is on, a long press turns the group off and cancels the timer

Retrigger Timer

If the group is off, a short press turns on the group at the specified level with the specified ramp
rate and starts the timer

At the expiry of the timer, the expiry event is generated

If the group is on, a short press turns the group off with the same ramp rate as the initial event

Toggle Timer

A short press starts the timer, without generating an event
Delay Timer At the expiry of the timer, the expiry event is generated
A long press does nothing

A short press sends the initial event, regardless of group state, and starts the timer

Pulse Timer A long press does nothing

Toggle Timer with On The same as Toggle Timer, but a long press turns the group on without starting the timer

Wall Plates: Linked Widget
The Linked Widget allows control of a group based on an event on another Widget (the
Control widget).

The Linked Widget uses a Linked Event on the Control Widget to trigger a Linked Action on its own
group.

part of the Linked profile, key 1 will have the following programming:

   •    Linked Event: Control Widget turns on
   •    Linked Action: Turn On

Wall Plates: Temperature & Humidity
The Saturn & Saturn Zen Wall Plates have integrated temperature & relative humidity sensors, where
the measured values are broadcast on the Measurement Application. The temperature and humidity
measurements can be independently configured to broadcast at regular intervals and/or upon a
change of reading.


          Relative humidity will be defined as a measurement of the amount of moisture in the
          air, relative to the maximum possible amount of moisture that the air can hold at the
          current temperature. Accurate relative humidity readings therefore require accurate
                                   adjustment of the temperature offset.


Alarms are also configurable for both high and low conditions for temperature and humidity. Alarms
can be set to turn on Lighting Groups when the temperature or humidity crosses the configurable
alarm threshold values. Each of these high/low/temperature/humidity alarms can be enabled or
disabled per profile. Note that once an alarm set threshold has been crossed and the alarm group has
been switched on, disabling that alarm event with a Profile change will still allow the alarm group to be
switched off when the alarm clear threshold is crossed.




                              Figure 284: Saturn Zen Wall Plate Measurement category


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property           Page | 155
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
It is recommended that the unit is installed in its final mounting location and left powered for at least 1
hour in order for the temperature to stabilise.

The temperature of the environment must also be stable, as the reading from the sensor can lag
changes in the environment by up to 10 minutes, depending on airflow.

As highlighted in the picture, a Temperature Offset field can be used to help offset the temperature
reading. This can be used in situations where the temperature that is being recorded by the Wall
Plate is not accurately reflecting the conditions of the location; therefore, helping to provide an
additional level of calibration.

C-Bus DALI-2: Scan DALI Line
The physical DALI line A will now be opened & scanned, the following scans are available in
SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission:

DALI Scan
A DALI Scan is the default scan performed for a DALI Line to discover devices on the DALI Line and
address any unaddressed devices, while also identifying any duplicate, missing and broken DALI
devices. This scan also includes the scan of extended DALI information; namely, the DALI Group
Membership information, with resulting information for each device displayed in the Line Devices
section.
Objective
Continue working with DALI devices to configure DALI Group Memberships, in addition to sorting out
the DALI Line (address and/or readdress devices).
Main Use Cases
1) Configure DALI Group Memberships of DALI devices on the DALI Line.
2) Discover DALI devices.
3) Sort out the DALI Line (address and/or readdress devices).




                                                Figure 330: DALI Scan

DALI Device Type Scan
A DALI Device Type scan is a basic DALI Line scan to discover devices on the DALI Line and address
any unaddressed devices, while also identifying any duplicate, missing and broken DALI devices.
From this scan, information for each device in the Line Devices section is displayed except for DALI
Group Membership information.

Objective
Get started with DALI devices and sort out a DALI Line where extended DALI information is not
required/needed for the task at hand.

Main Use Cases
1) Discover DALI devices.
2) Sort out the DALI Line (address and/or readdress devices).




                                          Figure 331: DALI Device Type Scan


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property      Page | 180
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Read DALI Gateway
A Read DALI Gateway scan retrieves the information stored in the C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway live on the
network, including the C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway programming and the programming of all DALI Devices
(according to the C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway).

Objective
The main objective of a Read DALI Gateway scan is to rebuild a project from a live/existing C-Bus
DALI-2 Gateway on the network which is already programmed/configured (and operational)
where a C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway does not exist in the project. This scan shall extract all the
information within the C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway and include all the information presently known about
the DALI devices on the lines of the Gateway. The Read DALI Gateway scan does not perform any
new device discovery, nor does it discover or resolve faults, and does not readdress any unaddressed
DALI devices.

Main Use Cases
1) The installer has lost their project and needs to recover the entire C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway
programming including the programming of all DALI Devices (according to the C-Bus DALI-2
Gateway).
2) The installer wishes to transfer the live C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway back from live network to the project
to ensure their project is up to date and current.
Note:
• Since SLCC since v2.9.0, a Read DALI Gateway scan is no longer mandatory when working with
    live DALI devices and C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway. Performing certain software operations, such as
    Loading a DALI device into the Property Editor or Extracting a single DALI device to the project is
    now possible without the dependency on a Read DALI Gateway scan.
• A DALI Scan and/or a DALI Device Type Scan is a required prerequisite when working "live" to
    discover new devices, discover faults and readdress any unaddressed devices (which updates the
    C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway).
• Since SLCC v2.9.0, the tooltips for each of the scan options have been enhanced to better
    describe the scan function.




                                           Figure 332: Read DALI Gateway

   1. This example will use the DALI Scan, click DALI Scan.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 181
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

C-Bus DALI-2: C-Bus to CCT
It was highlighted earlier that if a C-Bus group address is assigned only to the CCT/Hue Group
parameter and the Saturation Group parameter is left Unused, the object will be treated as a CCT
object. C-Bus Commands to this group will be mapped to control the Colour Temperature of this
DALI object.

For CCT DALI objects, the level of the C-Bus group address that is assigned to the CCT/Hue group
parameter can be mapped to a fixed CCT value in Kelvin (K) using the following equation:




                          Figure 361: DALI Group 1 - CCT/Hue Group group address added


                            CCT (K) = 1000 + (50 × C-Bus Group Address Level)

A C-Bus group address (0 - 255) can represent CCT values from 1000K to 13750K in 50K steps, a
table is provided below which displays C-Bus levels that correspond to common CCT values:




For different CCT devices, the range of warm & cool colours may differ; in situations where you have
devices on the same DALI group that have different warm and cool limitations, the C-Bus DALI-2
Gateway will limit the minimum and maximum CCT values sent to that group to a value that is
achievable by all members.

If this happens, and DALI to C-Bus mapping is enabled, this new value will be sent back onto C-Bus
to help ensure consistency.


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 198
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
C-Bus DALI-2: C-Bus to Hue/Saturation
It was highlighted earlier that if a C-Bus group address is assigned to both the CCT/Hue Group
parameter and Saturation Group parameters, the object will be treated as an RGB/RGBW object.
C-Bus Commands to the C-Bus group addresses will be mapped to control the Hue & Saturation of
this DALI object respectively.

When the Saturation Group parameter has a group address assigned an additional parameter, RGB
Mode is made available, giving you the capability to set whether the DALI Object is treated as an RGB
or RGBW object.

For RGB/RGBW DALI objects, the level of the C-Bus group controlling Hue is mapped to a Hue value
of 0 - 360° using the equation:
                                                      𝐶−𝐵𝑢𝑠 𝐺𝑟𝑜𝑢𝑝 𝐴𝑑𝑑𝑟𝑒𝑠𝑠 𝐿𝑒𝑣𝑒𝑙
                                      Hue = 360 × (                                )
                                                                  255

Some Hue values for common colours and their corresponding C-Bus levels and nearest C-Bus % are
shown in the table below:




The level of the Saturation group maps to the Saturation level as per the normal C-Bus level to
percentage mapping where 255 = 100%.

Setting the Saturation level to zero will result in white, regardless of the Hue value.

The DALI-2 compliant DT8 driver is responsible for keeping the colour constant as the level is dimmed,
so brightness changes via the Brightness group should not affect the colour.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property          Page | 199
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
  1. Navigate to C-Bus Toolkit and Refresh the project:,




                                     Figure 362: Refresh C-Bus Toolkit Project


  2. Navigate to the TRAINING project and open the Graphical User Interface for the Saturn Zen

C-Bus DALI-2: Missing Devices
The Properties of the SpaceLogic C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway includes a Missing Device Threshold
parameter for each DALI Line:




                     Figure 379: SpaceLogic C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway Missing Device Threshold


As part of its general operation, the C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway will poll each DALI device on each DALI
line. During this poll, the gateway will verify the status of each device, helping it determine whether the
device is available/ok or missing. This polling is done sequentially, determined by the short address
of each device, with the number of devices on the line influencing how long the total process will take
from the first device to the last. Once the gateway has polled the status of the last device on the line it
will then start the cycle again from the first device.

Each device on the line, will have its own missing device count, as seen by the gateway. During the
poll, if the status of a device is missing, the count will increase by 1. As the name suggests, the
Missing Device Threshold parameter highlighted earlier determines when the C-Bus DALI-2 gateway
will recognize a device as missing; for this example, the threshold has been set to 2; therefore, if a
device's missing device count reaches 2, the gateway will officially recognize that device as missing.
Once the gateway recognizes that any device on the line is missing, the Install Indicator button on
the front of the gateway will change to yellow.

This will be concept in the next exercise.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property            Page | 213
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

C-Bus DALI-2: Discover Faults
The Discover Faults scan is a scan to discover and reveal any DALI ECG devices on the DALI line that
might have the same Short Address (Duplicate), report as Missing or report as Broken, in
circumstances where their Fault Status as known by the C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway and displayed in the
software is not yet reflecting the physical environment. This scan is a dedicated scan which can take
some time depending on the number of devices on the DALI line and is usually run when the User
suspects a fault condition with DALI ECG devices on the DALI line which is not reflected either by the
C-Bus DALI-2 Gateway front panel LEDs or by the C-Bus Commission software.

Discover Faults
The Discover Faults scan is a scan to discover and reveal any DALI devices found on the DALI Line
that might have a conflicting Short Address (Duplicate), or might be Missing (no comms) or might be
Broken (hardware fault).

Objective
The Discover Faults scan is a dedicated scan run in circumstances where a fault condition is
suspected with DALI devices on the DALI Line, which are not reflected either by the C-Bus DALI-2
Gateway front panel LEDs or by the DALI device Fault Status in the C-Bus Commission software.
Note:
• The Discover Faults scan is a detailed scan and may take some time to complete depending on
   the number of devices on the DALI Line.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property       Page | 218
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Even Extracting from Network Devices to Devices in Project does not create the IP Layer (This can
only be done by adding a controller from the library).

The properties of each controller layer are as follows:
   • The IP Layer will not have any address, serial, firmware version or unit type and cannot be
       readdressed
   • The IP Layer cannot be readdressed
   • When loading the IP Layer into the Properties window, the Deploy to Network option is
       disabled
   • The SYS and PC Layer is used to configure, reconcile and deploy to the device
   • The SYS and PC Layer cannot be deleted independently

The following is a suggested workflow to help configure the properties of a Controller in a SpaceLogic
C-Bus Commission project:

   1. Add the appropriate controller from the library to Devices in Project, creating the 3 layers of
      IP, PC & SYS
   2. Open and Scan the C-Bus Network, the controller will appear only as PC & SYS
   3. Change the Unit Address of both the PC and SYS Layer on the live network to match their
      project device equivalents
   4. Extract both the PC and SYS Layers from the live network to the project

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.

C-Bus Controllers: Objects
In the C-Bus Controllers, group addresses are represented using the term, Objects. These objects
represent different C-Bus applications for sending and receiving values, forming the basis for all
communication between the group addresses and the controller:




                                      Figure 411: C-Bus Controller - Objects tab


Each group address is represented as an individual object as part of the Lighting Application,
which can be monitored, exported for communication with an external building management system
via BACnet IP, logged to have its Current value tracked or have an Event-based script
linked/associated to it.


          The capabilities of each object may vary depending on which Application Address it
             belongs to, some of these different application addresses have already been
                            explored, such as the Measurement application.

            By treating each group address as an object, the Current Value of each group
          address can be expanded beyond C-Bus and used in conjunction with other 3rd party
                                             protocols.

C-Bus Controllers: Controller Sync
In SpaceLogic C-Bus Commission, the C-Bus Controllers support a feature known as Controller
Sync, which consists of 2 functions:
    • Transfer: The function is used to push a designated Object List from the project to the
       controller
    • Retrieve: The function is used to pull the data back from the controller and store the data in
       the project
The Controller Sync feature is available in the IP Layer of the controller:




                                Figure 425: Controller Configuration - Controller Sync

An Object List (highlighted below) is the defined list of Object/s that:
   • Can be transferred to an appropriately synced controller
   • Have been received by an appropriately synced controller




                                           Figure 426: Controller Object List



 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 239
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
When transferring to the C-Bus Controller, the following content will be sent*:
  • Object List
  • The C-Bus Applications data (Tag Map):
       o Lighting Application
       o Trigger Control Application
       o Enable Application
       o Error Application
       o Emergency & Exit Lighting Application
       o Measurement Application

The scope of contents is the same when retrieving from the C-Bus Controller to the project.
*Only missing content is created and existing content updated (Names)

C-Bus Controllers: Circadian Rhythm
Circadian rhythm is defined as an 24-hour internal body block that helps to regulate sleep & wake
cycles, hormone release, digestion and other biological processes.

This rhythm can be regulated through the surrounding environmental light by mimicking natural light
patterns.

Using brighter / cooler light during the day can help maintain or improve focus, whilst using dimmer /
warmer light during the evening can help the body to wind down before bed.

The 2 × CCT LED strips and C-Bus Controller in the demo case will now be used in an exercise to
implement an example circadian rhythm lighting schedule:

                 Time of Day                 Colour Temperature (K)
                 6am                         2700K
                 7am                         3400K
                 8am                         4000K
                 9am                         4600K
                 10am                        5300K
                 11am                        6000K
                 12pm                        6500K
                 1pm                         6000K
                 2pm                         5300K
                 3pm                         4600K
                 4pm                         4000K
                 5pm                         3400K
                 6pm                         2700K


The C-Bus DALI-2: C-Bus to CCT chapter highlighted that for CCT DALI objects, the level of the C-
Bus group address that is assigned to the CCT/Hue group parameter can be mapped to a fixed CCT
value in Kelvin (K) using the following equation:

                            CCT (K) = 1000 + (50 × C-Bus Group Address Level)

In this example, the group address used to adjust the colour temperature is: DG1A_CCT.



 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property       Page | 248
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

C-Bus Controllers: BACnet
BACnet is designed to allow communication of building automation and control systems for
applications such as heating, ventilation, air conditioning control, lighting control, access control, fire
detection systems and their associated equipment. The BACnet protocol helps to provide exchange
information for building automation devices, regardless of the particular building service they perform.

There are different formats for BACnet: BACnet MS/TP, BACnet Ethernet and BACnet IP. Both
formats use Ethernet medium for communications; however, the 5500NAC2 and 5500AC2 only
supports the BACnet IP format via the RJ45 Ethernet connector.

In this manner, the controllers support the capability to act only as a BACnet server, exporting data
which can be read by BACnet client devices (like Building Management Systems).

The C-Bus Controller in the demo case will now have its BACnet server functionality enabled:

   1. In the Utilities Page, click the System button.




                                        Figure 467: C-Bus Controller - System

   2. The System Configuration Page will open in a new browser tab.




                              Figure 468: C-Bus Controller - System Configuration Page


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property             Page | 260
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
   3. Hover the mouse over Network in the provided menu bar, displaying a drop-down list.
   4. Click BACnet Settings.




                                   Figure 469: C-Bus Controller - BACnet settings


The BACnet settings dialog box appears.




                                       Figure 470: BACnet settings dialog box




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property    Page | 261
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Manager & Manager Config
The Manager Config application gives you the capability to create a visual structure / representation of
a controller project without the need for customized graphics, such as background images or floor
plans. This visual structure is then used by the Manager application or Clipsal C-Bus Manager to
help provide front-end control through a desktop or smart phone respectively:




          Figure 481: C-Bus Controller Manager Application and Clipsal C-Bus Manager for smart phones

The Controllers also support a customisable visualisation interface that can be accessed on:
   • Computer/Laptop
   • Tablet/iPad
   • Appropriate Smart Phone

This provides the end user with a visual medium to:
   • Control their C-Bus lighting through assorted control icons
   • Trigger different scenes from a single control icon
   • View live readings from connected meters such as power consumption
   • View created trend logs and/or schedules
   • Execute emergency and exit light testing and reporting (5500NAC2 only)

C-Bus Controllers: Function & Duration Test
The C-Bus Network Automation Controller (5500NAC2) supports the capability to perform Function &
Duration Testing of emergency & exit lights.

The following definitions for each test will be used for this workbook:
   • Function Test: The function test can be considered as a basic functionality test, checking that
        the major components of an emergency light are in place and working.

   •    Duration Test: The duration test performs the same checks as the function test; however, they
        are performed over the expected duration of time of the emergency fitting.

For example, if the standard duration time in a commercial building is 90 minutes, this means that the
fitting must keep its lamp illuminated without external power for this length of time (at a minimum).


          The rated duration of Emergency & Exit fittings are determined by local standards for
              the respective country, it is recommended to refer to standards organisation
           documentation for the initial commissioning time value and the subsequent testing
                                      time period for regular testing.


This exercise will cover the procedure of how to perform a function and duration Test using the C-Bus
Controller and the test group that was transferred from the TRAINING project in C-Bus Commission.

Once a function and duration test is completed, a test report is generated providing information such
as the date & time the test occurred, the result, site ID, and an Admin Logo:

   •    Site ID: The Site ID field represents the physical building in which the emergency and exit
        light/s are installed, it is recommended to use the name of the overall building as the Site ID as
        it will be displayed on all emergency and exit lighting test reports.

   •    Admin Logo: This logo will be displayed on the reports run using the Administrator account.

A function test will now be performed on the available emergency light.

   4. Ensure that the emergency light is connected to the demo case.
   5. Navigate to the C-Bus Controller Utilities Page.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property            Page | 314
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

C-Bus Controllers: C-Bus Scenes & Hue / Saturation
The exercise Manager Config: CCT & Hue/Saturation introduced using widgets (specifically the
Lighting Presets widget) to control the colour temperature & Hue of the DALI fittings in the demo
case, applying it for notable days such as Remembrance and R U OK? day:




                                   Figure 588: Notable Days Lighting Preset Widget

This extension exercise will build on this concept using Scenes and the Scene Widget in the
C-Bus Controller to implement for the following behaviour in the demo case:


 Remembrance Day
 DALI fittings                   eDLT Background & Indicator                       Saturn Zen Black Wall Plate
             's → Red                                &            → Red                    → Red



 R U OK? Day
 DALI fittings                   eDLT Background & Indicator                       Saturn Zen Black Wall Plate
             's → Yellow                             &            → Yellow                 → Yellow

Recall that the colour change of the RGBW LED's in the demo case is already controlled using the
C-Bus group address, DG2A_Hue; therefore, there is no need to perform any additional programming
from this perspective.

The Background & Indicator colour of the Saturn Zen eDLT can be changed using two C-Bus group
addresses respectively, whilst the ndicator colour of the Saturn Zen black wall plate can be changed
by changing the wall plate's current profile.

   •    If its level is below 34% (85), set the background colour to Red

Wall Plates: Join Room
It was highlighted in the Wall Plates: Profiles exercise that the profiles feature in the Saturn & Saturn
Zen Wall Plates can be used to replicate the Join Room functionality of older key input units. This
exercise will focus on programming a Join Room feature for 2 × Wall Plates, based on the following
real-world application:




                                           Figure 650: Join Room Scenario

There are two rooms, R1 and R2, each with 2 × Lighting Circuits, Front & Back. Each lighting circuit
is wired to a channel of a 4 Channel Voltage Free Relay. This can be represented using the relay in
the demo case.

The Wall Plate in each room will be presented by the Saturn Zen White & Black Wall Plates
respectively, each operating using 2 Profiles via the Enable group address, Function Room Profiles:

Function Room Profiles (254/203/2)
   • Level (0 - 0%) Profile 1 — Normal
   • Level (1) Profile 2 — Join Room

A widget will also be added to the C-Bus Controller to provide toggle functionality to switch the profile
of each wall plate to Join Room.


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property            Page | 347
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

Modbus RTU

Modbus is an open communication protocol that allows communication between industrial electronic
devices such as energy meters, available in two versions based on the network being used:

Modbus over serial (RTU) communication uses a physical layer for devices to communicate, in this
application is implemented through RS-485 wiring protocols.

A Modbus RTU configuration consists of two or more Modbus devices connected using a trunk cable,
also known as a bus.

One device acts as a Leader with the remaining devices acting as Followers, communication is
performed by the leader sending and receiving request messages (also known as commands) to the
followers on the bus. The followers are recognised through a Modbus Address which can be set on
each device:

Modbus RTU
Leader Device Follower Device 1 Follower Device 2
Modbus Address 1 Modbus Address 2

Figure 693: Modbus RTU

A Modbus RTU bus has the following properties:

A Bus can have a maximum of 32 devices (1 Leader and 31 Followers)

A Leader does not have a Modbus Address on the Modbus Network

A Follower device can have a Modbus Address within the range: 1 - 247

The Modbus devices can be connected directly on the bus, known as a Daisy Chain topology
The rate at which information is transmitted across the Modbus RTU bus is known as the
Baud rate

The Leader and Follower devices will have an error-check setting known as Parity. It performs
a check to help ensure that the sent information in a command, from the leader, matches the
information received from a command by the follower.

For a Modbus RTU connection connected in a Daisy Chain, it is important that all
devices have:

* a Unique Modbus Address

* the same Baud Rate and Parity setings

This is to help ensure the devices communicate correctly on the Modbus network.
Modbus TCP/IP
In this application it is implemented using TCP/IP over an Ethernet network:




                                              Figure 694: Modbus TCP/IP


Modbus over TCP/IP communication is done between a device acting as a Client, and another acting
as a Server. To establish the network connection, a Client will request a connection from the Server,
if the Server acknowledges the request, a connection is then established.

Modbus TCP/IP has the following properties:
  • Modbus TCP/IP operates on a network using an Ethernet connection
  • Devices are identified through an IP Address and a Modbus Address
  • When the client sends a request to a server, the request includes a destination port on the
     server. For a connection to be established the port on that server must be enabled/open
  • The Default Modbus TCP Port Number is 502, it is important that this port is enabled on the
     Client and Server devices


             For some instances, port 502 may be closed due to the Firewall settings of the
                                   connected computer or network.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property        Page | 375
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Modbus Registers
The information in a Modbus device is stored in different locations throughout its memory, these
locations are known as Modbus Registers. Consider an iEM3150 energy meter, the user manual for
the energy meter contains a section labelled Modbus register list:




                                      Figure 695: Example Modbus Register List


From the table shown above, different information about the energy meter is stored in different
registers; for example, the Serial Number can be found in register 130.


            The C-Bus Controllers may not be able to read all information found in a device.

            For non-Schneider products it is recommended to contact the manufacturer if this
                                     information cannot be found


When communication is established between the C-Bus Controller and a device operating on Modbus,
the controller can read the information from each of the registers and map them to an object; however,
this is influenced by the device's Modbus Profile.


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property         Page | 376
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Modbus: Profiles
A Modbus profile is a *.json file with a structure which defines how to read from and write to registers
in a Modbus device. This *.json file defines the Modbus registers and their corresponding parameters
in a manner that allows the C-Bus COntroller to read, interpret and map the stored information into an
object.




                                    Figure 696: C-Bus Controller & Modbus Profile

The C-Bus Controllers natively supports a list of Modbus profiles for an array of Schneider Electric
devices:




                          Figure 697: C-Bus Controller natively supported Modbus Profiles




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property            Page | 377
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
           This training will not cover procedures on how to create a Modbus profile, this is
         because each device may have its own unique properties and/or registers; therefore,
           the procedure to create a Modbus profile for one deice may not be applicable for
                                                another

of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

C-Bus Controllers: Scripting Engine
In commercial or residential applications, tasks and operations can be automated to execute at certain
times or when certain conditions are met. This type of application can be programmed in other C-Bus
products, such as using logic in C-Bus Touchscreens; however, it is also achievable in the
5500NAC2 and 5500AC2, by writing and executing blocks of code (known as scripts).

Through scripting, the controllers can potentially achieve functionality beyond what is possible with
C-Bus, this includes, but is not limited to:
   • Integration to 3rd Party Devices via IP and Serial
   • Keyword control
   • Aggregation of data

The 5500NAC2/5500AC2 uses the programming language, Lua, to perform some of their logic-based
functionality. The controllers contain an internal script editor, allowing a user to create and edit different
scripts to achieve their desired functionality, this is accessible through the Scripting tab in the
controllers' internal web server:




                                               Figure 751: Scripting tab

The controllers support six scripting types:
   • Event-based
   • Resident
   • Scheduled
   • User libraries
   • Common functions
   • Start-up (init) script

Each scripting type supports different parameters, such as:
   • Sleep interval
   • Time execution
   • Linked object

These parameters help to influence how a script will behave and what conditions are required for
correct functionality.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property              Page | 402
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
C-Bus Controllers: Script Editor
All scripts can be edited by the user using the internal script editor. The script editor will be displayed in
the same format, regardless of the scripting type:




                                               Figure 752: Script Editor

There are numerous concepts associated with the Script Editor in the C-Bus Controllers, to help
demonstrate these concepts consider an example script below:




                                              Figure 753: Example Script

The next page will explore the different concepts of what components help to form a script.


 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property              Page | 403
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Comments
Comments are lines of code which are not executed when the script is run. They are used to provide
an explanation of the code to the user. Comments are created by inserting '--' before the line of code
and formatted as orange in the Script Editor by the Automation Controller. An example of a comment
can be seen in Line 1 of the example script.




                                                 Figure 754: Comment

Variable
In a script, a variable is considered as a container where data can be stored. This data can be the the
information returned from a function, numerical data or a line of text (string). Variables can be
referenced in later sections of the code to access the data contained within. In the example code, a
variable: message can be seen in Line 9 which contains the string: 'Light has been set to 50%'. The
contents of that variable is accessed in Line 15 by a log function.



                                                  Figure 755: Variable

String
A String can be considered as a sequence of characters or line of text in the Script Editor. In the
Automation Controller, a string is defined in red and can be initialized by enclosing a line of text using
single quotes: ' ', double quotes: " " or two square brackets: [[ ]].



                                             Figure 756: String Example 1




                                             Figure 757: String Example 2

Numbers
The term Numbers will be used to represent any numerical value. In the Automation Controller, a
number defined in green and can be used to represent different values in the Automation Controller. In
Line 6 the integer, 50 represents a percentage, whilst in Line 12, the integer, 4 represents a ramp rate
in seconds. They can also be used to represent the different addresses which are supported by
C-Bus.


                                                 Figure 758: Numbers




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property            Page | 404
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Functions
In Lua, a function can be considered as a grouping mechanism for a section of code, if called, it will
execute the section of code it contains. A majority of functions in Lua can be generalized with the
following coding structure:




                                            Figure 759: function structure

   •    Initialization: functions are declared using the term function, followed by the function name
        and completed by the term end. These two terms encase the section of code which is
        contained within the function.
   •    Argument: A function can take in a single argument or multiple arguments; data such as
        numbers or a string which are used during the execution of the code contained within. It is not
        mandatory for functions to have arguments, some functions can operate without any
        arguments.
   •    Return: Once all the relevant code has been inserted, adding in the code line: return will
        cause the function to return any value/s once it has completed executing. Examples of
        returnable values could be the contents of a variable such as a string or numerical value.


          It is not Mandatory for a function to return anything once it has completed executing


The Example Script shows a function with the function name: Light_To_50pct, initialized on Line 3
and completed on Line 17. The function takes in one argument, the variable, Group and does not
return anything after execution.

A function can be accessed (known as called) by a script to utilize the section of code contained
within. This means a user does not have to repeat and re-write the same section of code multiple
times, the user can call the function by referencing its function name.


            To help ensure that a function can be accessed by a script, it is recommended to
             declare and insert that function at the top of the script before any other code is
            written. If the function is declared in later sections of the script, then it will not be
                                  accessible by any preceding lines of code.




 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property                 Page | 405
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Scripting: Event-based Script Example
In the C-Bus Controllers, an Event-based script is a script that is linked to an Object or Keyword, the
script is triggered each time that object or object associated with that keyword updates its Current
value.

           Recall that in the C-Bus Controllers, group addresses are represented as Objects.

            These objects represent different C-Bus applications for sending and receiving
           values, forming the basis for all communication between the group addresses and
                                               the controller.

Weather API: Sample Script
    1. Copy and paste the following script into Line 1 and save the changes:

require('json')
require("ltn12")
require('ssl.https')
local locationID = 'Insert Location here'
local apikey = 'Insert API Key as per Service Provider'

url = "https://api.weatherapi.com/v1/forecast.json?key="..apikey.."&q="..locationID.."&days=1&aqi=no&alerts=no"

data, error = ssl.https.request(url)

data = json.pdecode(data)
if not data then
alert('Weather: cannot parse data')
return
end
log(data)

--Grab Minimum Temperature in Degrees Celcius
min_temp = data.forecast.forecastday[1].day.mintemp_c

--Grab Maximum Temperature in Degrees Celcius
max_temp = data.forecast.forecastday[1].day.maxtemp_c

--Set Min/Max Temp & Icon Code into Objects (0 represents ℃)
SetCBusMeasurement('Local', 'Controller', 200, min_temp, 0)
SetCBusMeasurement('Local', 'Controller', 201, max_temp, 0)




                                        Figure 794: Weather API Script Editor



 © 2026 Schneider Electric. Life Is On | Clipsal is a trademark and the property               Page | 422
 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
Weather API: Sample Script Functionality
This script has been designed to operate in the following manner:
   • Poll / Request an external 3rd party weather service provider / database for a table of weather
       information
            o The number of times this script can poll / request fully depends on the subscription that
               the customer has with the 3rd party weather database.

   •    Once the poll has been done, this table in stored in the variable, data
   •    Functions in the script are used to pull the information from this table
           o Function pulls Daily Forecast of Minimum Temperature and stores it in the
               Measurement object (0/228/6/200)
           o Function pulls Daily Forecast of Maximum Temperature and stores it in the
               Measurement object (0/228/6/201)


                         The script polls the API using Line 7, from a specific URL




                                        Figure 795: Weather API Script Editor


The url that the script uses to poll the API requires:
   • Location ID also known as Location Key
   • API Key as per the service provider

You can either build your own url using the variables locationID, & apikey, or have one
generated for you by the service provider.

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

   •    If description = 'Cloudy' then set value in User Parameter to 7
   •    If description = 'Overcast' then set value in User Parameter to 9
   •    If description = 'Light rain' then set value in User Parameter to 11

        SetUserParam('Local', 257, 4)
end

        SetUserParam('Local', 257, 7)
end

if(descr == 'Overcast') then
        SetUserParam('Local', 257, 9)
end

--If Text Description is 'Light rain' set User Parameter object to 11 (to show sun icon)
if(descr == 'Light rain') then
        SetUserParam('Local', 257, 11)
end

FAQ’s

Phantom Group Address

Recall that in C-Bus, the Status Report (also known as Multipoint to Multipoint Interrogation) is a
reporting technique used to help ensure that all group addresses on a single given C-Bus network are
in sync with each other.

If the Status Report detects that group addresses are out of sync, the units on the network will
automatically self-correct the discrepancy. A Status Report occurs every 3 seconds by default, this
means that if a discrepancy is found it will be corrected within a few Status Report cycles.

The Status Report is generated for every Lighting application that exists on a C-Bus network. Some
C-Bus units may initiate Status Reports regardless of which application address they are using. When
a Status Report is initiated, units will buffer any C-Bus commands until the Status Report has
completed its error checking for that particular application.

There may be scenarios where a group address exists on an Input Unit but not on any Output Unit,
such as using a lighting group address to enable or disable the occupancy sensor in a C-Bus sensor:

o SENPILLA - Unit in Database at Address 5 (Network Not Open) X

Figure 840: PIR Sensor - Occupancy Enable/Disable
As the group address: Meeting Room Occupancy Sensor is not programmed in any output units it may
not synchronise properly with the other group addresses on the network. To help avoid this, the group
address could be programmed as a Logic Group in a Relay or Dimmer:




                                       Figure 841: Relay - Logic Group enabled

Once the group address has been programmed as a logic group, the Logic checkbox can be disabled,
removing the logic functionality but retaining the group address in the output unit:




                                       Figure 842: Relay - Logic Group disabled


                  This practice was commonly referred to as a Phantom Group Address

 of Schneider Electric SE, its subsidiaries and affiliated companies.
 All other trademarks are the property of their respective owners.
